   25: # SQL comparison operators that are safe to interpolate into WHERE clauses.
   26: # Scalar-binding operators: the value is always passed as a bind param (?), so
   27: # no quoting or escaping is required on the value side.  LIKE and NOT LIKE are
   28: # safe for the same reason â€” the pattern is bound, not interpolated.
   29: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_SQL_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!=', '=', 'LIKE', 'NOT LIKE');
   30: 
   31: # List-membership operators whose criterion value is an arrayref.
   32: # Each element is bound as a separate ?, so injection is impossible.
   33: # IN  with an empty list â†’ WHERE 1=0 (no rows match, correct SQL semantics).
   34: # NOT IN with an empty list â†’ no WHERE term (all rows match, correct semantics).
   35: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_LIST_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IN', 'NOT IN');
   36: 
   37: # Nullability operators: no bind parameter.  The hashref value is ignored â€”
   38: # any value (undef, 1, ...) signals intent; only the key selects the operator.
   39: # A bare undef criterion value (col => undef) also generates IS NULL.
   40: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_NOARG_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IS NULL', 'IS NOT NULL');

  107: 
  108: =head1 NAME
  109: 
  110: Database::Join - Read-only combined view across two or more Database::Abstraction objects
  111: 
  112: =head1 VERSION
  113: 
  114: Version 0.010.0
  115: 
  116: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  117: 
  118: B<Basic two-database join>
  119: 
  120:     use Database::Join;
  121: 
  122:     # Step 1: create each component database the normal way
  123:     my $customers = Database::Customers->new(directory => '/data');
  124:     my $loyalty   = Database::Loyalty->new(directory  => '/data');
  125: 
  126:     # Step 2: combine them on the shared key column 'entry'
  127:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  128:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  129:         join_column => 'entry',
  130:     );
  131: 
  132:     # Step 3: query exactly as you would a single Database::Abstraction object
  133:     my $all_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  134:     my $vip_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
  135:     my $one_row   = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
  136:     my $total     = $join->count();
  137:     my $col_names = $join->columns();
  138: 
  139: B<Hiding internal columns>
  140: 
  141:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  142:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  143:         join_column    => 'entry',
  144:         remove_columns => [ 'internal_id', 'audit_ts' ],
  145:     );
  146:     # 'internal_id' and 'audit_ts' never appear in results or columns()
  147: 
  148: B<join_map: when the key column has different names in each database>
  149: 
  150:     # $cities  (index 0) has a column called 'statecode' -- matches join_column
  151:     # $stnames (index 1) has a column called 'entry'     -- different name
  152: 
  153:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  154:         databases   => [ $cities,  $stnames ],
  155:         #                index 0   index 1
  156:         join_column => 'statecode',
  157:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },  # index 1 calls its join key 'entry'
  158:     );
  159: 
  160:     # All returned rows use 'statecode'; 'entry' is never exposed
  161:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  162: 
  163: B<filters: permanently restrict a database's visible rows>
  164: 
  165:     # Only show orders placed more than 60 days ago, without repeating
  166:     # the criterion on every query call.
  167:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  168:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
  169:         join_column => 'entry',
  170:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
  171:     );
  172: 
  173:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();                 # all old orders
  174:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');   # old + gold tier
  175: 
  176: B<Inner and outer join types>
  177: 
  178:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
  179:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  180:         join_column => 'entry',
  181:         join_type   => 'inner',   # only keys present in BOTH databases
  182:     );
  183: 
  184:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
  185:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  186:         join_column => 'entry',
  187:         join_type   => 'outer',   # all keys from EITHER database
  188:     );
  189: 
  190: B<Building the view incrementally with add_database>
  191: 
  192:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  193:         databases   => [ $customers ],
  194:         join_column => 'entry',
  195:     );
  196: 
  197:     $join->add_database($loyalty)
  198:          ->add_database($scores, remove_columns => ['raw_score']);
  199: 
  200: B<AUTOLOAD column shortcut>
  201: 
  202:     # Returns the 'name' value for entry 'C001' (scalar context)
  203:     my $name = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
  204: 
  205:     # Returns all 'tier' values (list context)
  206:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
  207: 
  208: B<aggregate(): GROUP BY across the merged view>
  209: 
  210:     # Count and total score per tier, highest total first
  211:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
  212:         by       => ['tier'],
  213:         measures => [
  214:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
  215:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
  216:         ],
  217:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
  218:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
  219:     );
  220:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
  221: 
  222:     # Grand total across the whole view (no 'by' key)
  223:     my $summary = $join->aggregate(
  224:         measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }],
  225:     );
  226:     # => [ { n => 42 } ]
  227: 
  228: B<SQLite join backend for large datasets>
  229: 
  230:     # 'auto' (default): switches to SQLite automatically above the threshold
  231:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  232:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  233:         join_column    => 'entry',
  234:         backend        => 'auto',          # default
  235:         max_array_rows => 50_000,          # use SQLite when combined rows > 50,000
  236:     );
  237: 
  238:     # Always use SQLite -- useful when you know the data is large
  239:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  240:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  241:         join_column => 'entry',
  242:         backend     => 'sqlite',
  243:         tmpdir      => '/fast/nvme/tmp',   # optional: faster temp disk
  244:     );
  245: 
  246:     # Always use the original in-memory path
  247:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  248:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  249:         join_column => 'entry',
  250:         backend     => 'array',
  251:     );
  252: 
  253: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  254: 
  255: C<Database::Join> merges two or more L<Database::Abstraction> objects into a
  256: single logical, read-only view.  Each component database is queried
  257: independently through its own C<Database::Abstraction> interface.  The results
  258: are combined using a shared key column (C<join_column>).
  259: In effect, this means that you can view data from more than one database using
  260: an intuitive, non-SQL interface.
  261: 
  262: Every storage format that C<Database::Abstraction> supports works as a
  263: component database: CSV, PSV, TSV, SQLite, JSON, XML, XLSX, BerkeleyDB, HTML
  264: URL, JSON URL, or any custom subclass.  Component databases may mix formats
  265: within the same join.
  266: 
  267: The module exposes the same read-only API as C<Database::Abstraction>:
  268: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>,
  269: C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>, C<set_logger>, and the AUTOLOAD column
  270: shortcut.  Callers do not need to know how many underlying databases are
  271: involved.
  272: 
  273: Think of it as a virtual database table that is assembled on demand from
  274: several real tables, one per component database.
  275: 
  276: B<Join backends>
  277: 
  278: By default (C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>), C<Database::Join> first checks the
  279: combined source row count.  For small datasets (up to C<max_array_rows>,
  280: default 10,000 rows) it merges entirely in Perl memory.  For larger datasets it
  281: automatically spills source rows into a temporary SQLite database and executes
  282: a single SQL JOIN there, keeping peak RAM to roughly one times the source data
  283: size instead of three.  You can also force either path unconditionally with
  284: C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>.
  285: 
  286: =head2 Join semantics
  287: 
  288: The C<join_type> parameter controls what happens when a particular key value
  289: exists in some component databases but not all:
  290: 
  291: =over 4
  292: 
  293: =item C<left> (the default)
  294: 
  295: All rows from the I<primary> (first) database are returned.  Columns from
  296: subsequent databases are included where a matching row is found, and simply
  297: absent from the hashref where there is no match.  If you are familiar with
  298: SQL, this is a LEFT OUTER JOIN on the first table.
  299: 
  300: =item C<inner>
  301: 
  302: Only rows whose join-column value is present in I<every> component database
  303: are returned.  This is equivalent to a SQL INNER JOIN.
  304: 
  305: =item C<outer>
  306: 
  307: Every join-column value found in I<any> component database is returned.
  308: Columns from databases that do not have that key value are absent from the
  309: merged row.  This is a FULL OUTER JOIN.
  310: 
  311: =back
  312: 
  313: B<Important override rule:> whenever you pass a query criterion for a column
  314: that belongs to a secondary database, that database automatically acts as an
  315: inner-join partner for that query only -- regardless of C<join_type>.  This
  316: gives WHERE-clause semantics.  For example, if you have a LEFT join but query
  317: C<< tier => 'gold' >> on a secondary database, only rows whose secondary entry
  318: has tier = 'gold' are returned (rows with no secondary entry are excluded, just
  319: as a WHERE clause would exclude them).
  320: 
  321: =head2 Column ownership and routing
  322: 
  323: At construction time, C<Database::Join> calls C<columns()> on each component
  324: database and builds an internal index that maps every column name to the
  325: database that owns it.
  326: 
  327: When you pass criteria to a query method, each key-value pair is automatically
  328: routed to the right database.  You never need to say which database a column
  329: belongs to.
  330: 
  331: The C<join_column> is special: criteria on it are broadcast to I<all>
  332: databases so that each database fetches only the relevant rows before the
  333: in-memory merge.
  334: 
  335: When the same non-join column name exists in more than one database, the
  336: I<last> database in the C<databases> array wins by default: its value
  337: overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.  Use C<collision_prefix> to
  338: preserve both values under distinct names instead.
  339: 
  340: =head1 LIMITATIONS
  341: 
  342: =over 4
  343: 
  344: =item Memory usage (array backend)
  345: 
  346: When C<backend> is C<'array'> (or C<'auto'> and the dataset is small), all
  347: matching rows are fetched into Perl memory.  Peak RAM is roughly three times
  348: the source data size.  For large datasets use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>, or
  349: leave C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> and set C<max_array_rows> appropriately.
  350: 
  351: =item SQLite backend uses a persistent cache file
  352: 
  353: When the SQLite path is active, a single C<.db> file with a randomly generated
  354: name (chosen by C<File::Temp> to avoid collisions) is created in C<tmpdir> the
  355: first time a query runs on a given C<Database::Join> object.  Source data is
  356: spilled into that file once; subsequent queries against the same object reuse
  357: the file without re-fetching the source data.
  358: 
  359: The cache is automatically invalidated and rebuilt whenever any source
  360: database's C<updated()> timestamp changes (indicating new data), or when
  361: C<add_database()> is called.
  362: 
  363: The file is deleted when the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (typically
  364: when it goes out of scope).  At any given moment no more than one such file
  365: exists per object.  The directory must be writable and have enough free space
  366: for the full source data (once, not per-query).
  367: 
  368: =item No chained builder or raw SQL
  369: 
  370: C<query()> and C<execute()> are not implemented.  Use C<selectall_arrayref>
  371: or C<fetchrow_hashref> instead.
  372: 
  373: =item Single-column equi-join only
  374: 
  375: Joining on more than one column simultaneously, or on expressions, is not
  376: supported.  When the join key has different names in different databases,
  377: use C<join_map> to declare each database's local column name.
  378: 
  379: =item Sort order
  380: 
  381: Results are sorted ascending by C<join_column> by default.  Pass
  382: C<< sort_by => 'colname' >> (or C<< sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC'] >>)
  383: to any query method to override this.  The array path uses string comparison
  384: (C<cmp>); for accurate numeric ordering on large datasets use the SQLite
  385: backend, which sorts natively by type.
  386: 
  387: =item count() on the array backend fetches all rows
  388: 
  389: On the array backend, C<count()> executes the full in-memory join and counts
  390: the resulting rows in Perl; no C<COUNT(*)> is pushed to the component
  391: databases.  On the SQLite backend, C<count()> executes a C<SELECT COUNT(*)>
  392: SQL query against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row transfer.
  393: 
  394: =back
  395: 
  396: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
  397: 
  398: =over 4
  399: 
  400: =item The join_column must exist in every component database
  401: 
  402: If even one database is missing the join key column, C<new()> (or
  403: C<add_database()>) will C<croak> immediately.  Use C<join_map> when the
  404: column has a different local name in some databases.
  405: 
  406: =item Criteria on a removed column are silently dropped
  407: 
  408: If you call C<remove_column('tier')> and later query
  409: C<< selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold') >>, the criterion is ignored (with a
  410: C<carp> warning) and all rows are returned.  Always pass criteria before
  411: removing columns, or restructure your code to avoid this.
  412: 
  413: =item You cannot remove the join column
  414: 
  415: C<< $join->remove_column($join->join_column) >> will C<croak>.  The join key
  416: is required for the merge to work.
  417: 
  418: =item Left join does not guarantee all columns are populated
  419: 
  420: Under a LEFT join, rows from the primary database that have no matching row
  421: in a secondary database will be returned with I<no keys> from that secondary
  422: database.  Accessing C<< $row->{score} >> on such a row returns C<undef> --
  423: not zero, not an empty string.  Always test C<defined $row->{score}> rather
  424: than just C<$row->{score}> when the secondary match is optional.
  425: 
  426: =item Filters act as inner-join partners
  427: 
  428: Any database that has a C<filters> entry is promoted to an inner-join partner,
  429: regardless of C<join_type>.  A row whose join-key value does not appear in
  430: the filtered database's result is removed from the merged output entirely, not
  431: merely missing its secondary columns.  This is intentional but can be
  432: surprising if you expected LEFT join semantics.
  433: 
  434: =item Criteria-merging replaces scalar filters
  435: 
  436: When both a base filter and a query criterion target the same column, and both
  437: are operator hashrefs (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >>), the operators are combined
  438: (AND semantics).  But if the query criterion is a plain scalar (e.g.
  439: C<< score => 75 >>), it I<replaces> the base filter for that column entirely --
  440: the base filter is ignored for that query.
  441: 
  442: =item AUTOLOAD sees the full merged join when filters or join_map are active
  443: 
  444: When either C<filters> or C<join_map> is in effect, the AUTOLOAD shortcut
  445: (C<< $join->columnname(...) >>) runs the full join query rather than
  446: delegating directly to the owning database.  This is necessary for correctness
  447: but means the result respects all active filters and join-key translations,
  448: which may differ from what the owning database would return on its own.
  449: 
  450: =item Duplicate column names: last database wins (unless collision_prefix is set)
  451: 
  452: When two component databases each have a column called C<notes>, the second
  453: database's value silently overwrites the first in every merged row.  Use
  454: C<collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' }> to publish the second database's
  455: C<notes> as C<right.notes> so both values survive, or use C<remove_columns>
  456: (or C<remove_column>) to drop the unwanted duplicate entirely.
  457: 
  458: =item Mutating the filters hashref after construction has no effect
  459: 
  460: C<Database::Join> deep-copies the C<filters> hashref (and any C<filter>
  461: passed to C<add_database>) at the moment of construction.  The original hashref
  462: you passed in is never stored.  If you later modify it -- for example, to
  463: tighten or loosen a filter criterion -- the joined view is I<not> affected.
  464: Construct a new C<Database::Join> object, or use a component
  465: C<Database::Abstraction> that supports dynamic filter modification.
  466: 
  467: =item auto mode may always use the array path for some DAs
  468: 
  469: C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> counts rows cheaply only when each component database
  470: either implements C<dbi_source()> (SQLite-backed) or directly defines a
  471: C<count()> method in its own package.  A DA that merely I<inherits> C<count()>
  472: from C<Database::Abstraction> is treated as uncountable, because the parent
  473: class C<count()> expects a key argument and behaves differently from a
  474: "return total row count" function.  In that case C<Database::Join>
  475: conservatively uses the array path for the whole query, even if the dataset is
  476: large.  To opt in to the SQLite path for such a DA, either add your own
  477: C<count()> override that returns the total row count, implement C<dbi_source()>,
  478: or use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> unconditionally.
  479: 
  480: =item dbi_source() ATTACH is unconditional - query-time criteria go into WHERE
  481: 
  482: When a component database implements C<dbi_source()>, C<Database::Join> always
  483: uses the zero-copy ATTACH path, I<even when the current query includes criteria
  484: for columns in that database>.  The criteria are translated into parameterised
  485: SQL C<WHERE> clauses applied against the ATTACHed table; no row-level copy is
  486: performed.  (Prior to 0.005.0 the presence of any query-time criteria would
  487: force a spill; that restriction has been removed.)
  488: 
  489: =item Broadcast join-column criterion does not force secondaries into inner-join
  490: 
  491: When a caller passes a join-column criterion (e.g. C<entry =E<gt> 'k1'>),
  492: C<Database::Join> broadcasts it to all component databases so each DA can
  493: filter its fetch to the requested key.  Prior to 0.006.0 this broadcast was
  494: incorrectly counted as "having criteria" for secondary databases, causing
  495: C<left> and C<outer> joins to silently behave as C<inner> joins when a
  496: join-column criterion was present.  The fix: only non-join-column criteria
  497: (e.g. column filters from the caller or base C<filters =E<gt> {...}>) promote
  498: a secondary to inner-join status.  The broadcast itself is now a transparent
  499: key-range selector that does not affect join semantics.
  500: 
  501: =item LIKE and NOT LIKE work on the SQLite path; other pattern operators do not yet
  502: 
  503: C<LIKE>, C<NOT LIKE>, C<IN>, and C<NOT IN> are fully supported on the SQLite
  504: backend.  C<LIKE>/C<NOT LIKE> take a scalar pattern; C<IN>/C<NOT IN> take an
  505: arrayref of values.  All are injection-safe because values are passed as bind
  506: parameters, never interpolated.
  507: 
  508:     # LIKE
  509:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(name => { LIKE => 'A%' });
  510: 
  511:     # IN
  512:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { IN => ['gold', 'silver'] });
  513: 
  514:     # NOT IN
  515:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { 'NOT IN' => ['bronze'] });
  516: 
  517: C<IN> with an empty arrayref matches no rows (SQL semantics: C<IN ()> is
  518: always false).  C<NOT IN> with an empty arrayref matches all rows (no
  519: constraint added).
  520: 
  521: C<IS NULL> and C<IS NOT NULL> are supported on the SQLite backend using an
  522: explicit operator hashref:
  523: 
  524:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NULL'     => undef });
  525:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NOT NULL' => 1    });
  526: 
  527: The hashref value is ignored; only the key selects the operator.  A bare
  528: C<undef> criterion value (C<< score => undef >>) also generates C<IS NULL>
  529: on the SQLite path.  Note: on the in-memory array path, the component DA
  530: may treat an C<undef> criterion value as C<< no filter >> rather than
  531: C<IS NULL>, so use the explicit hashref form for consistent behaviour
  532: across backends.
  533: 
  534: Any operator not in the supported set is skipped on the SQLite path with a
  535: C<carp> warning.  The array path forwards all operators to the component DA
  536: unchanged, which may or may not honour them.
  537: 
  538: =item Temp file directory must be writable and have free space
  539: 
  540: The SQLite path creates one temporary C<.db> file per C<Database::Join> object
  541: in C<tmpdir> (default: C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()>, usually C</tmp> on Unix).
  542: The filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp> -- you cannot predict it,
  543: only the directory is under your control.  The file is created on the first
  544: query and kept alive until the object is destroyed; it is not re-created on
  545: every query call.  If the directory is not writable, or the filesystem is full,
  546: the call will C<croak> with C<error_sqlite_connect>.  Check permissions and
  547: free space if you see that error.
  548: 
  549: =item C<join =E<gt>> criteria are not supported
  550: 
  551: C<Database::Abstraction> accepts a C<join =E<gt> { table =E<gt> ..., on =E<gt> ... }>
  552: key in its criteria hashrefs to express an SQL JOIN within a single table.
  553: C<Database::Join> cannot route this to a component DA meaningfully: the merged
  554: view has no concept of a single underlying table.  Passing C<join =E<gt>> to any
  555: query method will C<croak> with C<error_join_criterion>.
  556: 
  557: B<Fix:> Model the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object and
  558: add it to the C<databases =E<gt> []> list.  Use C<join_column> or C<join_map> to
  559: specify the shared key.
  560: 
  561: =item aggregate(): omitting C<by> gives a grand total, not an error
  562: 
  563: Calling C<aggregate()> without a C<by> key (or with C<< by => [] >>) does
  564: I<not> croak.  It returns a single-element arrayref containing one row with
  565: all the computed measure aliases.  This is a grand total over the entire view
  566: (possibly filtered by any extra criteria you pass).
  567: 
  568:     my $total = $join->aggregate(measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }]);
  569:     # $total->[0]{n} is the total row count
  570: 
  571: If you expected multiple rows and got one, you probably forgot the C<by> key.
  572: 
  573: =item aggregate(): non-numeric values behave differently across backends
  574: 
  575: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), any non-numeric value in a
  576: column targeted by C<sum> or C<avg> is I<skipped> (not counted, not coerced),
  577: and a C<carp> warning C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> is emitted.  On the SQLite
  578: backend, non-numeric values are silently coerced to C<0.0> via SQL C<CAST>.
  579: The two backends therefore produce different numeric totals when the data
  580: contains non-numeric values.  Clean your data first if you need consistent
  581: results across backends, or choose a backend explicitly.
  582: 
  583: =item aggregate(): C<BETWEEN> is not supported in C<having>
  584: 
  585: The C<having> hashref uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>,
  586: which does not include C<BETWEEN>.  Write a range as two explicit constraints:
  587: 
  588:     # Correct
  589:     having => { total => { '>=' => 10, '<=' => 50 } }
  590: 
  591:     # Wrong -- will croak with error_aggregate_having_operator
  592:     having => { total => { BETWEEN => [10, 50] } }
  593: 
  594: =item aggregate(): C<count> without C<col> counts all rows, not distinct non-NULL values
  595: 
  596: A measure with C<< fn => 'count' >> and no C<col> key emits C<COUNT(*)> on the
  597: SQLite backend (or counts every row in Perl).  This counts rows that have
  598: C<undef>/NULL in other columns too.  To count only rows where a specific
  599: column is non-NULL, or to count distinct values, use C<count_distinct>:
  600: 
  601:     # Count rows with a non-NULL 'score' (one way -- use a pre-filter)
  602:     measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'distinct_scores' }]
  603: 
  604: =item aggregate(): the result is never cached
  605: 
  606: Unlike C<selectall_arrayref> (which benefits from the SQLite file cache on
  607: repeated identical calls), C<aggregate()> always re-executes.  If your
  608: aggregation is expensive and the data changes infrequently, cache the result
  609: yourself using C<updated()> as a staleness signal.
  610: 
  611: =item distinct => 1 without columns returns full rows, not a column projection
  612: 
  613: C<< distinct => 1 >> alone deduplicates on I<all> columns of the merged row.
  614: If you want a distinct list of values for a single column (e.g. all unique
  615: tiers), you B<must> supply C<< columns => ['tier'] >> as well.  Without
  616: C<columns>, every column participates in the key used to detect duplicates,
  617: which means two rows that differ in any column (e.g. C<entry>) are never
  618: considered duplicates, and the result will be the same as without C<distinct>.
  619: 
  620: =item distinct => 1 with columns projects rows to those columns
  621: 
  622: When C<columns> is supplied alongside C<distinct>, each returned row contains
  623: I<only> the named columns -- all other columns are dropped.  The projection
  624: happens before deduplication, so the result set may be smaller than the full
  625: merged row count.  If you need the full row I<and> want to know unique values,
  626: run two separate queries or post-process in Perl.
  627: 
  628: =item mandatory_filters must never come from user-supplied input
  629: 
  630: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> form an enforcement boundary.  They must
  631: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
  632: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
  633: HTTP headers, or other user-controlled input.  A hostile user who can control
  634: a mandatory_filters predicate can widen it to C<{ col => 'status', op => 'ne',
  635: val => 'NONEXISTENT' }> and receive all rows regardless of status.
  636: 
  637: =item mandatory_filters uses string comparison on the array path
  638: 
  639: On the array backend, all C<mandatory_filters> comparisons (including C<lt>,
  640: C<lte>, C<gt>, C<gte>) are I<lexicographic string comparisons>, not numeric.
  641: C<< { col => 'score', op => 'gt', val => 9 } >> will match a score of C<'10'>
  642: only if C<'10' gt '9'> is true -- which it is not (C<'1' lt '9'>).  Use the
  643: SQLite backend (C<< backend => 'sqlite' >>) when numeric comparisons are needed,
  644: as SQLite uses the column's declared type for ordering.
  645: 
  646: =back
  647: 
  648: =head1 METHODS
  649: 
  650: =head2 new
  651: 
  652: =head3 SYNOPSIS
  653: 
  654:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  655:         databases         => [ $db1, $db2 ],
  656:         join_column       => 'entry',
  657:         join_type         => 'left',
  658:         join_map          => { 1 => 'local_col' },
  659:         filters           => { 1 => { score => { '>' => 60 } } },
  660:         base_criteria     => { active => 1 },
  661:         mandatory_filters => [{ col => 'region', op => 'eq', val => 'West' }],
  662:         collision_prefix  => { 1 => 'right' },
  663:         remove_columns    => [ 'email', 'internal_id' ],
  664:         backend           => 'auto',        # 'auto' | 'sqlite' | 'array'
  665:         max_array_rows    => 10_000,        # threshold for 'auto' mode
  666:         tmpdir            => '/tmp',        # directory for temp SQLite file
  667:         logger            => $log,
  668:         i18n              => $locale,
  669:     );
  670: 
  671: =head3 DESCRIPTION
  672: 
  673: Constructs and returns a new C<Database::Join> object.
  674: 
  675: Each element of C<databases> must be an already-instantiated subclass of
  676: C<Database::Abstraction>.  The constructor calls C<columns()> on every
  677: database to build an internal column-routing table and verifies that
  678: C<join_column> (or its local alias from C<join_map>) is present in each one.
  679: 
  680: Columns listed in C<remove_columns> are hidden immediately: they do not appear
  681: in C<columns()>, C<schema()>, or any returned row hashref.  This is equivalent
  682: to calling C<remove_column> once per name after construction.
  683: 
  684: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
  685: 
  686: =head4 Input
  687: 
  688:     databases      => { type => 'arrayref', required => 1 }
  689:                       # One or more Database::Abstraction subclass objects.
  690:                       #
  691:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   non-empty arrayref of blessed DA subclasses.
  692:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: scalar, hashref, or absent => croak.
  693:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA size:   minimum 1 element; no documented upper bound.
  694:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA elem:   each element must pass isa('Database::Abstraction').
  695: 
  696:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'entry' }
  697:                       # The column name shared by all databases (the join key).
  698:                       #
  699:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-empty string present in every component DA.
  700:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: column absent from any DA => croak join_col_missing.
  701:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        empty string '' is treated as a column name and
  702:                       #                       will croak if (as expected) it is absent from every DA.
  703:                       # DOMAIN -- NOTE:       matching is case-sensitive and exact.
  704: 
  705:     join_type      => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'left',
  706:                         enum => ['inner', 'left', 'outer'] }
  707:                       # Controls which keys appear in the result when not all
  708:                       # databases share the same key values.
  709:                       #
  710:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   exactly 'inner', 'left', or 'outer'.
  711:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string including 'INNER', 'LEFT',
  712:                       #                       'OUTER' (enum check is case-sensitive), 'cross',
  713:                       #                       or '' => croak from validate_strict.
  714: 
  715:     join_map       => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  716:                       # Zero-based database index => local column name.
  717:                       # See the join_map section for full details.
  718:                       #
  719:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref values must be plain strings.
  720:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: reference value (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.)
  721:                       #                       => croak; the guard prevents heap-address leakage.
  722:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        out-of-range keys (beyond the databases array) are
  723:                       #                       silently ignored.
  724: 
  725:     filters        => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  726:                       # Zero-based database index => criteria hashref.
  727:                       # Permanent row restrictions on individual databases.
  728:                       # See the filters section for full details.
  729: 
  730:     base_criteria  => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  731:                       # Column name => value hashref.
  732:                       # Permanent criteria applied to every query, specified by
  733:                       # column name rather than database index.  Each key must
  734:                       # be a column that appears in the merged view (or the
  735:                       # join_column); each value is a plain scalar or an
  736:                       # operator hashref in the same format as
  737:                       # selectall_arrayref accepts.  Criteria are automatically
  738:                       # routed to the database that owns each column (same
  739:                       # routing used by selectall_arrayref).
  740:                       # Equivalent to filters but more convenient when you know
  741:                       # the column name but not which database index owns it.
  742:                       # When a column appears in both base_criteria and filters,
  743:                       # the filters entry takes precedence.
  744:                       # See the base_criteria section for full details.
  745:                       #
  746:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of column-name => scalar or
  747:                       #                       operator-hashref pairs.  Unknown column
  748:                       #                       names emit warn_unknown_column (carp)
  749:                       #                       and are silently dropped.
  750:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-hashref value => croak from
  751:                       #                       validate_strict.
  752:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        {} empty hashref is a safe no-op.
  753: 
  754:     collision_prefix => { type => 'hashref', optional => 1 }
  755:                       # Zero-based database index (>0) => prefix string.
  756:                       # When a secondary database has a column that collides
  757:                       # with a column already present in the merged view, the
  758:                       # secondary column is published as "$prefix.$col" instead
  759:                       # of silently overwriting the earlier value.
  760:                       # Index 0 entries are silently ignored.
  761:                       # Omitting this parameter preserves the original
  762:                       # last-database-wins behaviour.
  763:                       # See the collision_prefix section for full details.
  764:                       #
  765:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   absent or {} => last-database-wins (no change).
  766:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   { N => 'prefix' } where N > 0 => colliding
  767:                       #                       columns from DB[N] published as "$prefix.$col";
  768:                       #                       non-colliding columns from the same DB added plain.
  769:                       # DOMAIN -- EP note:    index-0 entries are silently ignored.
  770:                       # DOMAIN -- Invariant:  join_column is never prefixed regardless of
  771:                       #                       collision_prefix configuration.
  772: 
  773:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  774:                       # Column names to hide from the merged view.
  775:                       #
  776:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of any strings; non-existent columns
  777:                       #                       are silently ignored (idempotent).
  778:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak remove_join_col.
  779:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        [] empty arrayref is a safe no-op.
  780: 
  781:     backend        => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'auto',
  782:                         enum => ['array', 'sqlite', 'auto'] }
  783:                       # Controls which join strategy is used.
  784:                       #   'auto'   -- (default) use 'array' when combined source row count
  785:                       #              <= max_array_rows, 'sqlite' otherwise.
  786:                       #   'sqlite' -- always spill to a temporary SQLite database.
  787:                       #   'array'  -- always use the in-memory merge path.
  788:                       #
  789:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto' (case-sensitive).
  790:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string => croak error_invalid_backend.
  791:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    'auto'.
  792: 
  793:     max_array_rows => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 10_000 }
  794:                       # Row-count threshold for 'auto' mode.  When the combined
  795:                       # source row count exceeds this value, the SQLite path is used.
  796:                       # Ignored when backend is 'array' or 'sqlite'.
  797:                       #
  798:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-negative integer.
  799:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        0 means always use SQLite (all counts exceed 0).
  800:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    10,000.
  801: 
  802:     tmpdir         => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  803:                       # Directory for the per-call temporary SQLite database file.
  804:                       # The file is created securely by File::Temp and removed when
  805:                       # the query completes.  Ignored when backend is 'array'.
  806:                       #
  807:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any writable directory path string.
  808:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  uses File::Spec->tmpdir() (system temp dir).
  809: 
  810:     parallel       => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 0 }
  811:                       # When set to 1 and the join has more than 2 databases (primary +
  812:                       # 2 or more secondaries), secondary DA fetches are issued in
  813:                       # parallel Perl threads.  Requires the 'threads' module; falls
  814:                       # back to sequential with a carp warning when unavailable.
  815:                       # Has no effect on the SQLite backend (which uses a single SQL
  816:                       # JOIN).  DBI-backed DAs are not thread-safe by default; only
  817:                       # enable this for in-memory or otherwise thread-safe DA backends.
  818:                       #
  819:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   0 (sequential, default) or 1 (parallel).
  820:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other integer is treated as truthy/falsy.
  821:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    0.
  822: 
  823:     autoload_pk     => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  824:                       # The column name used as the positional-argument key when
  825:                       # AUTOLOAD performs a full join query (i.e. when join_map or
  826:                       # filters is active).  Calling $join->col('value') with a bare
  827:                       # scalar maps that scalar to { autoload_pk => 'value' } before
  828:                       # routing through the merged view.
  829:                       #
  830:                       # Defaults to join_column when omitted.  Override when the
  831:                       # primary DA's natural lookup key differs from join_column â€”
  832:                       # for example, when join_column is 'statecode' but you want to
  833:                       # look up by city name ('entry').
  834:                       #
  835:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that is a visible column in
  836:                       #                       the merged view (checked at call time by
  837:                       #                       _partition_criteria; unknown names are
  838:                       #                       carp'd and dropped).
  839:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  defaults to join_column.
  840: 
  841:     mandatory_filters => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  842:                       # Arrayref of {col, op, val} predicate hashrefs.
  843:                       # These filters are applied to every query result regardless
  844:                       # of what criteria the caller supplies.  They cannot be
  845:                       # overridden, suppressed, or circumvented at query time.
  846:                       # Each predicate must supply:
  847:                       #   col -- a column name present in the merged view
  848:                       #   op  -- one of: eq, ne, lt, lte, gt, gte, like, contains
  849:                       #   val -- the comparison value (plain scalar)
  850:                       # On the array backend: applied as a Perl-side grep after the
  851:                       # in-memory merge, before sort/limit/offset.
  852:                       # On the SQLite backend: injected as parameterised WHERE clauses
  853:                       # into the per-call SQL query.
  854:                       #
  855:                       # SECURITY: Never set this from URL parameters or any
  856:                       # user-supplied value.  Predicates must come from the
  857:                       # server-side session or policy store only.
  858:                       #
  859:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   [] empty arrayref => no constraint (safe no-op).
  860:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of {col, op, val} hashrefs where
  861:                       #                       col is in the merged view and op is supported.
  862:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: element is not a hashref, or is missing
  863:                       #                       col/op/val => croak error_mandatory_filter_ref.
  864:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: col not in merged view => croak error_mandatory_filter_col.
  865:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: op not in supported set => croak error_mandatory_filter_op.
  866:                       # DOMAIN -- ops:        eq (string eq), ne (string ne),
  867:                       #                       lt/lte/gt/gte (string comparison),
  868:                       #                       like (SQL LIKE pattern with % and _),
  869:                       #                       contains (substring match, equivalent to LIKE %val%).
  870: 
  871:     logger         => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  872:                       # Logger object propagated to all component databases.
  873: 
  874:     i18n           => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  875:                       # Localisation object with a translate($key, @args) method.
  876: 
  877: =head4 Output
  878: 
  879:     A blessed Database::Join object.
  880: 
  881: =head3 EXAMPLE
  882: 
  883:     # Customers database: entry | name | email
  884:     # Loyalty   database: entry | tier | points
  885: 
  886:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  887:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  888:         join_column    => 'entry',
  889:         join_type      => 'inner',            # only customers who also have loyalty records
  890:         remove_columns => [ 'email' ],        # hide PII from query results
  891:         filters        => { 1 => { points => { '>' => 0 } } }, # ignore zero-point records
  892:     );
  893: 
  894:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  895:     # Each row: { entry => ..., name => ..., tier => ..., points => ... }
  896:     # 'email' is absent. Zero-point loyalty records are excluded.
  897: 
  898: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
  899: 
  900:     validate all parameters with validate_strict
  901:     croak if databases is empty
  902:     croak if any element of databases is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
  903:     bless the object with all fields initialised
  904:     call _build_col_index to map every column to its owning database
  905:         and verify join_column presence in each database
  906:     if base_criteria given:
  907:         partition base_criteria by column ownership into per-db slices
  908:         for each database with a non-empty slice:
  909:             merge the slice into _filters[i]
  910:             (explicit filters win on plain-scalar conflicts)
  911:     if mandatory_filters given:
  912:         for each predicate in mandatory_filters:
  913:             croak error_mandatory_filter_ref if predicate is not a {col, op, val} hashref
  914:             croak error_mandatory_filter_col if col is not in the merged view
  915:             croak error_mandatory_filter_op  if op is not in the supported set
  916:         store shallow copies of all validated predicates in _mandatory_filters
  917:     for each column in remove_columns: call remove_column
  918:     return the new object
  919: 
  920: =head3 MESSAGES
  921: 
  922:     error_no_databases           -- databases arrayref was empty
  923:     error_invalid_db             -- an element of databases is not a D::A subclass
  924:     error_join_col_missing       -- join_column (or its join_map alias) not found in a database
  925:     error_invalid_backend        -- backend value is not 'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto'
  926:     error_sqlite_connect         -- temporary SQLite database could not be created (backend='sqlite'/'auto')
  927:     error_mandatory_filter_ref   -- a mandatory_filters element is not a hashref with col, op, and val keys

  929:     error_mandatory_filter_op    -- a mandatory_filters element uses an unsupported op value
  930:     warn_schema_type_mismatch    -- (carp) a shared column has different types across databases;
  931:                                     use collision_prefix to preserve both values
  932: 
  933: =cut
