 1081: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1082: # Public API (mirrors Database::Abstraction)
 1083: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1084: 
 1085: =head2 join_map - joining on differently-named columns
 1086: 
 1087: By default every component database must have a column whose name matches
 1088: C<join_column>.  If a database uses a different local name for the join key,
 1089: declare the mapping with C<join_map>.
 1090: 
 1091: C<join_map> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1092: database in the C<databases> array (0 = first, 1 = second, and so on).  Each
 1093: B<value> is the name that B<that particular database> uses for the join key.
 1094: 
 1095: Databases not listed in C<join_map> are assumed to already have a column
 1096: named C<join_column> and need no entry.
 1097: 
 1098: Throughout the merged view the join key is I<always> referred to by the name
 1099: given in C<join_column>.  The local alias is never exposed in returned rows,
 1100: in C<columns()>, or in C<schema()>.
 1101: 
 1102: B<When do you need join_map?>
 1103: 
 1104: You need C<join_map> when you have two tables like:
 1105: 
 1106:     cities table  : entry (the city name) | statecode
 1107:     stnames table : entry (the state code) | state
 1108: 
 1109: Here you want to join cities.statecode to stnames.entry.  You choose
 1110: C<< join_column => 'statecode' >> as the canonical name, but stnames calls
 1111: that same concept C<entry>, so you declare:
 1112: 
 1113:     join_map => { 1 => 'entry' }  # stnames (index 1) calls it 'entry'
 1114: 
 1115: B<Example>
 1116: 
 1117:     #                        index 0     index 1
 1118:     my @databases = (       $cities,    $stnames  );
 1119:     #  join key column:    'statecode'  'entry'
 1120:     #  join_column:        'statecode' (chosen canonical name)
 1121:     #  stnames differs, so declare the alias:
 1122: 
 1123:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1124:         databases   => \@databases,
 1125:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1126:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },
 1127:     );
 1128: 
 1129:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1130:     # Each $row has keys: entry (city), statecode, state
 1131:     # 'entry' from stnames is never exposed directly.
 1132: 
 1133:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(statecode => 'CA');
 1134: 
 1135: B<Using add_database instead>
 1136: 
 1137: If you build the join incrementally with C<add_database>, pass
 1138: C<join_column> directly to that call instead of using C<join_map>:
 1139: 
 1140:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1141:         databases   => [ $cities ],
 1142:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1143:     );
 1144:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'entry');
 1145: 
 1146: This is exactly equivalent to the C<join_map> form above.
 1147: 
 1148: =head2 filters - permanent per-database row filters
 1149: 
 1150: C<filters> lets you restrict a component database to a subset of its rows
 1151: permanently, without repeating the criterion on every query call.
 1152: 
 1153: Think of it as telling the join: "whenever you query this database, always
 1154: add these extra conditions".  Callers never need to specify the restriction
 1155: themselves and can never accidentally omit it.
 1156: 
 1157: C<filters> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1158: database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).  Each
 1159: B<value> is a criteria hashref in the same format as C<selectall_arrayref>
 1160: accepts.
 1161: 
 1162: B<Key-set semantics>
 1163: 
 1164: A filtered database always acts as an inner-join partner, regardless of the
 1165: C<join_type> setting.  Any join-key value that does not pass the filter is
 1166: excluded from the merged output entirely -- not just missing its secondary
 1167: columns.  This ensures the filter genuinely restricts the view rather than
 1168: simply hiding a few fields.
 1169: 
 1170: B<Criteria merging>
 1171: 
 1172: When a query call also passes a criterion for a column that already has a base
 1173: filter, the two constraints are combined:
 1174: 
 1175: =over 4
 1176: 
 1177: =item *
 1178: 
 1179: When both the base filter value and the query criterion are operator hashrefs
 1180: (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >> and C<< { '<' => 365 } >>), their operators are
 1181: merged: I<both> constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 1182: 
 1183: =item *
 1184: 
 1185: When either value is a plain scalar, or the operators conflict, the
 1186: query-time criterion wins and the base filter for that column is ignored for
 1187: that one call.
 1188: 
 1189: =back
 1190: 
 1191: B<Example -- only show orders placed more than 60 days ago>
 1192: 
 1193:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1194:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1195:         join_column => 'entry',
 1196:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
 1197:     );
 1198: 
 1199:     # Every query automatically sees only old orders
 1200:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1201: 
 1202:     # Additional criteria layer on top -- gold tier AND old order
 1203:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1204: 
 1205:     # Range intersection: age_days > 60 AND age_days < 365
 1206:     my $mid  = $join->selectall_arrayref(age_days => { '<' => 365 });
 1207: 
 1208: When using C<add_database>, pass C<filter> (singular) to set the base
 1209: criteria for the new database:
 1210: 
 1211:     $join->add_database($orders, filter => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } });
 1212: 
 1213: =head2 base_criteria - permanent view-level row filters by column name
 1214: 
 1215: C<base_criteria> is a convenience alternative to C<filters> for callers who
 1216: know the column names they want to restrict but prefer not to track database
 1217: indices.
 1218: 
 1219:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1220:         databases     => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1221:         join_column   => 'entry',
 1222:         base_criteria => { active => 1, deleted_at => undef },
 1223:     );
 1224: 
 1225:     # Every query automatically sees only active, non-deleted rows.
 1226:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1227: 
 1228: At construction time C<base_criteria> is partitioned by column ownership
 1229: using the same routing logic as C<selectall_arrayref>.  Each criterion is
 1230: sent to the database that owns that column and stored as a permanent base
 1231: filter (equivalent to the corresponding C<filters> entry).
 1232: 
 1233: B<Unknown columns> emit a C<warn_unknown_column> carp and are silently
 1234: dropped, just as they would be in a query call.
 1235: 
 1236: B<Key-set semantics> are identical to C<filters>: any database that receives
 1237: a C<base_criteria> slice acts as an inner-join partner regardless of
 1238: C<join_type>.
 1239: 
 1240: B<Precedence>: when a column appears in both C<base_criteria> and C<filters>,
 1241: the C<filters> entry wins on plain-scalar conflicts; operator-hashref values
 1242: are combined with AND semantics.
 1243: 
 1244: B<Use cases>
 1245: 
 1246: =over 4
 1247: 
 1248: =item *
 1249: 
 1250: Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
 1251: 
 1252: =item *
 1253: 
 1254: Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
 1255: 
 1256: =item *
 1257: 
 1258: Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
 1259: 
 1260: =back
 1261: 
 1262: =head2 mandatory_filters - row-level security predicates that cannot be bypassed
 1263: 
 1264: C<mandatory_filters> is an arrayref of C<{col, op, val}> predicate hashrefs.
 1265: Every predicate is applied to the merged result of every query, regardless of
 1266: what criteria the caller passes.  Unlike C<filters> and C<base_criteria> (which
 1267: are per-database constraints applied at fetch time), C<mandatory_filters>
 1268: operates on the fully-merged row set, ensuring the constraint applies uniformly
 1269: even when the same logical value is spread across multiple databases.
 1270: 
 1271:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1272:         databases         => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 1273:         join_column       => 'entry',
 1274:         mandatory_filters => [
 1275:             { col => 'region',  op => 'eq',  val => 'West' },
 1276:             { col => 'tier',    op => 'ne',  val => 'banned' },
 1277:             { col => 'balance', op => 'gte', val => 0        },
 1278:         ],
 1279:     );
 1280: 
 1281: Each predicate hashref must supply exactly these three keys:
 1282: 
 1283: =over 4
 1284: 
 1285: =item C<col>
 1286: 
 1287: The column name in the merged view (as returned by C<columns()>).  Must be
 1288: present at construction time; columns added later via C<add_database()> are not
 1289: retroactively covered by predicates already in C<_mandatory_filters>.
 1290: 
 1291: =item C<op>
 1292: 
 1293: The comparison operator.  Supported values:
 1294: 
 1295:     eq       -- string equality      ($row->{col} eq $val)
 1296:     ne       -- string inequality    ($row->{col} ne $val)
 1297:     lt       -- string less-than     ($row->{col} lt $val)
 1298:     lte      -- string <=            ($row->{col} le $val)
 1299:     gt       -- string greater-than  ($row->{col} gt $val)
 1300:     gte      -- string >=            ($row->{col} ge $val)
 1301:     like     -- SQL LIKE pattern     (% = any sequence, _ = any one character)
 1302:     contains -- substring match      (row value must contain val as a substring)
 1303: 
 1304: Comparisons are lexicographic (string-based) on the array path; on the SQLite
 1305: path they follow SQLite's default collation for the column type.  Use the
 1306: SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) when numeric ordering matters.
 1307: 
 1308: =item C<val>
 1309: 
 1310: The right-hand side of the comparison.  Must be a plain scalar.
 1311: 
 1312: =back
 1313: 
 1314: B<Supported use cases>
 1315: 
 1316: =over 4
 1317: 
 1318: =item *
 1319: 
 1320: Row-level security: restrict the view to rows the current session is authorised
 1321: to see: C<< { col => 'tenant_id', op => 'eq', val => $session_tenant } >>
 1322: 
 1323: =item *
 1324: 
 1325: Data classification gates: hide rows marked with a sensitivity label:
 1326: C<< { col => 'classification', op => 'ne', val => 'restricted' } >>
 1327: 
 1328: =item *
 1329: 
 1330: Substring/pattern guards:
 1331: C<< { col => 'email', op => 'contains', val => '@example.com' } >>
 1332: or C<< { col => 'code', op => 'like', val => 'PROD_%' } >>
 1333: 
 1334: =back
 1335: 
 1336: B<SECURITY: C<mandatory_filters> must never be set from user-supplied values>
 1337: 
 1338: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> are an enforcement boundary.  They must
 1339: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
 1340: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
 1341: HTTP headers, or any other user-controlled input.  Setting this parameter from
 1342: user-supplied data would allow an attacker to widen or replace the constraints
 1343: and access rows they are not permitted to see.
 1344: 
 1345: B<Array vs. SQLite backend>
 1346: 
 1347: On the B<array backend>, C<mandatory_filters> is applied as a Perl-side C<grep>
 1348: after the in-memory merge, immediately before C<sort_by>/C<limit>/C<offset>.
 1349: A row that does not satisfy every predicate is silently dropped.
 1350: 
 1351: On the B<SQLite backend>, each predicate is translated into a parameterised
 1352: C<WHERE> clause and injected into the per-call SQL query.  Values are always
 1353: passed via DBI bind parameters, never by string interpolation.
 1354: 
 1355: Both backends produce identical filtered results.
 1356: 
 1357: =head2 distinct - deduplicate merged rows
 1358: 
 1359: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> to C<selectall_arrayref> or C<selectall_array> to
 1360: remove duplicate rows from the merged result.
 1361: 
 1362:     # All unique tier values
 1363:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1364:         distinct => 1,
 1365:         columns  => ['tier'],
 1366:     );
 1367: 
 1368:     # All unique region + tier combinations
 1369:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1370:         distinct => 1,
 1371:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1372:     );
 1373: 
 1374:     # Remove fully-identical merged rows (all columns participate)
 1375:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1376: 
 1377: The optional C<columns> arrayref projects each row to the named columns I<before>
 1378: deduplication.  Omitting C<columns> (or passing an empty arrayref) deduplicates
 1379: on the full merged row: two rows must be identical in every column to be
 1380: considered duplicates.
 1381: 
 1382: B<Array backend>: deduplication is done in Perl using a seen-hash keyed on the
 1383: selected column values.  First occurrence wins.
 1384: 
 1385: B<SQLite backend>: C<SELECT DISTINCT col1, col2, ...> is used; the SQL engine
 1386: handles deduplication.
 1387: 
 1388: Both backends produce identical results.
 1389: 
 1390: B<Interaction with sort_by / limit / offset>: C<distinct> is applied first
 1391: (before pagination and ordering), so C<limit> and C<offset> operate on the
 1392: already-deduplicated set.
 1393: 
 1394: =head2 collision_prefix - preserve colliding columns from secondary databases
 1395: 
 1396: By default, when a column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 1397: database wins: its value silently overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.
 1398: This loses data and makes the origin invisible.
 1399: 
 1400: C<collision_prefix> changes this for secondary databases you designate.
 1401: When a secondary database at index N has a column that already exists in the
 1402: merged view, and C<collision_prefix-E<gt>{N}> is set, the colliding column is
 1403: published as C<"$prefix.$col"> instead of overwriting.  Both values are then
 1404: visible: the original column keeps its name (from the earlier database), and
 1405: the collision gets the prefixed name.
 1406: 
 1407: C<collision_prefix> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based index> of
 1408: a secondary database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).
 1409: Each B<value> is the prefix string to prepend.  An index-0 entry is
 1410: meaningless and silently ignored.  Omitting C<collision_prefix> entirely
 1411: preserves the previous last-wins behaviour and changes nothing.
 1412: 
 1413: Non-colliding columns from a secondary database are always added as-is with
 1414: no prefix, whether or not C<collision_prefix> is configured.
 1415: 
 1416: B<Example -- sales table and products table, both with a "product" column>
 1417: 
 1418:     # $sales    columns: id, product, amount, date
 1419:     # $products columns: sku, product, price, category
 1420:     # join on 'product' (left key) matched against 'sku' (right key via join_map)
 1421: 
 1422:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1423:         databases        => [$sales, $products],
 1424:         join_column      => 'product',
 1425:         join_map         => { 1 => 'sku' },
 1426:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'products' },
 1427:     );
 1428: 
 1429:     $join->columns;
 1430:     # => ['amount', 'category', 'date', 'id', 'price', 'product', 'products.product']
 1431:     #                                                               ^-- prefixed collision
 1432: 
 1433:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(product => 'widget');
 1434:     # $row->{product}            -- value from $sales
 1435:     # $row->{'products.product'} -- value from $products (different row, same column name)
 1436:     # $row->{price}              -- from $products, no collision, kept as-is
 1437: 
 1438: B<Querying on a prefixed column>
 1439: 
 1440: Use the full published name as the criterion key:
 1441: 
 1442:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('products.product' => 'widget');
 1443:     # Internally routes as: product => 'widget' to $products
 1444: 
 1445: B<Interaction with remove_column>
 1446: 
 1447: C<remove_column> operates on published names.  To suppress a prefixed
 1448: collision column entirely, pass the prefixed name:
 1449: 
 1450:     $join->remove_column('products.product');
 1451: 
 1452: =head2 backend - SQLite join backend for large datasets
 1453: 
 1454: C<Database::Join> can merge component databases in two different ways,
 1455: controlled by the C<backend> constructor parameter.
 1456: 
 1457: =over 4
 1458: 
 1459: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'array'> -- in-memory merge (original behaviour)
 1460: 
 1461: All matching rows are fetched from every component database into Perl hashes
 1462: and merged there.  Simple and fast for small and medium datasets.  Peak RAM
 1463: is roughly three times the combined source data size (one copy per database
 1464: plus one merged copy).
 1465: 
 1466: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> -- SQL JOIN via a cached temporary file
 1467: 
 1468: C<Database::Join> creates a temporary SQLite database file, spills source
 1469: rows into it (one table per component database), then executes a single SQL
 1470: C<JOIN> statement per query call.  Peak RAM drops to roughly one times the
 1471: source data size.
 1472: 
 1473: The temporary file is created once and reused across multiple query calls on
 1474: the same object (the cache).  Only query-time criteria vary per call; they
 1475: are applied as SQL C<WHERE> clauses against the cached data.  The cache is
 1476: automatically rebuilt when any source database's C<updated()> timestamp
 1477: changes.  The file is deleted when the object is destroyed (goes out of
 1478: scope).  See I<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime> below for
 1479: details.
 1480: 
 1481: Requires C<DBD::SQLite E<gt>= 1.70> (C<FULL OUTER JOIN> support was added in
 1482: SQLite 3.39.0; DBD::SQLite 1.70 ships SQLite 3.39.2).
 1483: 
 1484: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> (default)
 1485: 
 1486: C<Database::Join> counts the total rows from all component databases cheaply
 1487: -- without fetching them -- and then decides:
 1488: 
 1489: =over 4
 1490: 
 1491: =item *
 1492: 
 1493: If the combined count is less than or equal to C<max_array_rows> (default
 1494: 10,000), use the array path.
 1495: 
 1496: =item *
 1497: 
 1498: If the combined count exceeds C<max_array_rows>, use the SQLite path.
 1499: 
 1500: =back
 1501: 
 1502: For counting to work without fetching, each component database must either
 1503: implement the C<dbi_source()> interface (for SQLite-backed sources, where a
 1504: C<COUNT(*)> SQL query is issued directly), or directly define a C<count()>
 1505: method in its own package -- not just inherit one from a parent class.  If
 1506: neither is available for a particular database, C<Database::Join> plays it
 1507: safe and uses the array path for the whole query without fetching any rows.
 1508: 
 1509: =back
 1510: 
 1511: B<Choosing max_array_rows>
 1512: 
 1513: The default of 10,000 is a reasonable starting point.  Adjust it to match
 1514: your hardware and typical row width.  For wide rows (many columns or long
 1515: strings) you may want a lower threshold; for narrow rows you can raise it.
 1516: 
 1517: B<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime>
 1518: 
 1519: When the SQLite path is active, a single temporary SQLite database file acts
 1520: as the join cache for the life of the C<Database::Join> object.
 1521: 
 1522: B<Name>: the filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp>, for example:
 1523: 
 1524:     /tmp/Cj8xK7mP2Q.db
 1525: 
 1526: The random portion (ten characters) is chosen automatically to avoid
 1527: collisions.  Only the directory is under your control; you cannot specify
 1528: the filename itself.
 1529: 
 1530: B<Location>: controlled by the C<tmpdir> constructor parameter.
 1531: If C<tmpdir> is not specified, C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()> is used (usually
 1532: C</tmp> on Unix, or the value of the C<TEMP> or C<TMP> environment variable
 1533: on Windows).
 1534: 
 1535: B<Lifetime: one file per object, deleted when the object is destroyed>: the
 1536: file is created on the first query call that uses the SQLite path and kept
 1537: alive until the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (i.e. when it goes out
 1538: of scope or is explicitly C<undef>-d).  At most one file exists per object at
 1539: any given moment.  Calling C<selectall_arrayref()> ten times on the same
 1540: object creates and uses I<one> file, not ten.
 1541: 
 1542: B<Cache invalidation>: the cache is automatically rebuilt (the old file is
 1543: replaced with a new one) when any source database's C<updated()> return value
 1544: changes, or when C<add_database()> is called.  Base-filter criteria
 1545: (C<filters> constructor parameter) are applied once at build time for spilled
 1546: sources; query-time criteria are applied per-call as SQL C<WHERE> clauses.
 1547: 
 1548: To use a different directory -- for example a RAM-backed filesystem or a
 1549: faster local disk:
 1550: 
 1551:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1552:         databases      => [ $db1, $db2 ],
 1553:         join_column    => 'entry',
 1554:         backend        => 'sqlite',
 1555:         tmpdir         => '/dev/shm',     # Linux RAM disk
 1556:     );
 1557: 
 1558: B<Parallel secondary fetches (C<parallel> constructor parameter)>
 1559: 
 1560: By default, component databases are queried sequentially - the primary first,
 1561: then each secondary in order.  When the component databases are network- or
 1562: disk-backed and have non-trivial per-query latency, the sequential fetch means
 1563: total latency is the I<sum> of all per-DA latencies.
 1564: 
 1565: Setting C<< parallel => 1 >> in the constructor enables concurrent fetching
 1566: of secondary databases using Perl C<threads>.  With C<parallel => 1> and
 1567: two or more secondary databases (C<n E<gt> 2> total), secondary fetches run in
 1568: parallel after the primary fetch completes; total latency drops to
 1569: I<max(secondary latencies)> instead of I<sum(secondary latencies)>.
 1570: 
 1571:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1572:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty, $scores ],   # 3 DAs - 2 secondaries
 1573:         join_column => 'entry',
 1574:         parallel    => 1,    # loyalty and scores fetched concurrently
 1575:     );
 1576: 
 1577: Requirements and caveats:
 1578: 
 1579: =over 4
 1580: 
 1581: =item *
 1582: 
 1583: The C<threads> module must be available.  Most distributions ship it, but it
 1584: requires a Perl binary compiled with C<-Dusethreads>.  When threads are
 1585: unavailable, a C<carp> warning is emitted and fetching falls back to
 1586: sequential; the result is identical, only slower.
 1587: 
 1588: =item *
 1589: 
 1590: Parallel fetching is only active when the join has three or more total
 1591: databases (C<n E<gt> 2>).  With two databases (one secondary), the thread
 1592: creation overhead exceeds the benefit of concurrency; sequential is used
 1593: regardless of C<parallel>.
 1594: 
 1595: =item *
 1596: 
 1597: Component databases must be safe to call from Perl threads.  In-memory
 1598: databases (CSV, JSON, TSV after slurp) are safe.  DBI-backed databases
 1599: whose handles were created in the same thread may not be safe - consult your
 1600: DBD driver's thread documentation.  The array backend is recommended for
 1601: DBI-backed sources; the SQLite backend performs its join in a single SQL
 1602: statement and does not use parallel fetching.
 1603: 
 1604: =item *
 1605: 
 1606: C<parallel => 1> applies to the array backend only.  The SQLite backend
 1607: performs a single SQL JOIN after spilling source data, so per-DA parallelism
 1608: is irrelevant.
 1609: 
 1610: =back
 1611: 
 1612: B<Zero-copy ATTACH (C<dbi_source()> interface)>
 1613: 
 1614: Normally, when the SQLite path is active, rows from each component database
 1615: are fetched one by one and inserted into the temporary SQLite file.  This is
 1616: efficient but does involve INSERT overhead.
 1617: 
 1618: If a component database is itself SQLite-backed and implements a
 1619: C<dbi_source()> method, C<Database::Join> can skip the row-by-row copy
 1620: entirely and instead use C<ATTACH DATABASE> to link the source file directly
 1621: to the temporary join connection.  This is the zero-copy path and is
 1622: significantly faster for large SQLite sources.
 1623: 
 1624: The C<dbi_source()> method must return a hashref with two keys:
 1625: 
 1626: =over 4
 1627: 
 1628: =item C<dbh>
 1629: 
 1630: A connected C<DBD::SQLite> database handle (C<DBI> connection object).
 1631: 
 1632: =item C<table>
 1633: 
 1634: The name of the table in that database that holds the source rows.
 1635: 
 1636: =back
 1637: 
 1638: Example implementation:
 1639: 
 1640:     package My::SQLiteDatabase;
 1641:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
 1642: 
 1643:     sub dbi_source {
 1644:         my ($self) = @_;
 1645:         return {
 1646:             dbh   => $self->{_dbh},      # connected DBD::SQLite handle
 1647:             table => $self->{_table},    # table name in that database
 1648:         };
 1649:     }
 1650: 
 1651:     1;
 1652: 
 1653: The zero-copy ATTACH path is always used when a component database implements
 1654: C<dbi_source()>.  Query-time criteria are applied as parameterised SQL
 1655: C<WHERE> clauses against the ATTACHed source table, so no row-level copy is
 1656: needed even when the current call includes column filters.  (Prior to 0.005.0
 1657: any query-time criteria forced a spill; that restriction was removed in
 1658: 0.005.0.)
 1659: 
 1660: B<Result identity>
 1661: 
 1662: Both the array path and the SQLite path produce identical results for any
 1663: given query.  You can switch between them freely without changing callers.
 1664: The C<collision_prefix> column renaming, C<join_map> key translation, and all
 1665: three join types (left, inner, outer) work identically on both paths.
 1666: 
 1667: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1668: 
 1669: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1670: 
 1671:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1672:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier  => 'gold');
 1673:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1674:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('C001');  # positional: entry => 'C001'
 1675:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1676:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1677:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10);
 1678:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1679:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => 'name', limit => 5);
 1680:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1681:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);  # distinct on all columns
 1682: 
 1683: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1684: 
 1685: Returns an arrayref of hashrefs representing the merged view of all component
 1686: databases, optionally filtered by the given criteria.
 1687: 
 1688: Criteria for columns that live in different databases are routed
 1689: automatically: each database is queried with only the criteria that apply to
 1690: its own columns.  The results are combined in memory using C<join_column>.
 1691: 
 1692: Accepts the same criteria syntax as C<Database::Abstraction::selectall_arrayref>.
 1693: A single plain scalar argument is interpreted as the C<join_column> value
 1694: (equivalent to C<< entry => 'C001' >> when C<join_column> is C<'entry'>).
 1695: 
 1696: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> (with optional C<< columns => \@cols >>) to
 1697: deduplicate results; see the C<distinct> feature section for details.
 1698: 
 1699: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1700: 
 1701: =head4 Input
 1702: 
 1703:     Calling conventions (in order of precedence):
 1704:       1. No arguments             -- returns all rows
 1705:       2. One plain scalar         -- shorthand for join_column => $scalar
 1706:       3. Key-value pairs or
 1707:          a criteria hashref       -- routed per-database
 1708: 
 1709:     Values may be:
 1710:       Plain scalar                -- exact match
 1711:       Hashref of operators        -- e.g. { '>' => 80 }
 1712: 
 1713:     Optional parameters (mixed in with any of the above):
 1714:       sort_by => 'colname'            -- sort ascending by that column
 1715:       sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC']  -- sort descending
 1716:       sort_by => ['colname', 'ASC']   -- sort ascending (explicit)
 1717:       limit    => N                    -- return at most N rows (positive integer)
 1718:       offset   => M                   -- skip the first M rows (non-negative integer)
 1719: 
 1720:     The column named in sort_by must be present in the merged view (i.e. it
 1721:     must appear in columns()).  An unknown column or an invalid direction emits
 1722:     a carp warning and falls back to the default join_column ascending sort.
 1723: 
 1724:     limit and offset are applied after ordering.  offset without limit skips
 1725:     rows but returns all remaining rows.  limit without offset starts from
 1726:     the first qualifying row.  An invalid limit or offset emits a carp warning
 1727:     and the parameter is ignored (treated as absent).
 1728: 
 1729:     DOMAIN -- sort_by:
 1730:       EP absent:          result sorted by join_column ASC (default).
 1731:       EP string:          any column name in columns(); sorts ASC by that column.
 1732:       EP ['col','ASC']:   explicit ascending; equivalent to the string form.
 1733:       EP ['col','DESC']:  descending sort by the named column.
 1734:       EP ['col']:         single-element arrayref; direction defaults to ASC.
 1735:       EP []:              empty arrayref; column is undef -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1736:       EP invalid column:  column not in columns() -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1737:       EP invalid dir:     direction not 'ASC' or 'DESC' -> carp + ASC used.
 1738:       Sort is lexicographic (cmp); use backend=>'sqlite' for numeric ORDER BY.
 1739: 
 1740:     DOMAIN -- limit:
 1741:       EP absent:          no truncation; all qualifying rows are returned.
 1742:       EP 0:               not a positive integer -> carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1743:       BVA min valid = 1:  exactly 1 row returned.
 1744:       BVA at count:       limit == total rows -> all rows returned (no truncation).
 1745:       BVA above count:    limit > total rows -> all rows returned.
 1746:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1747:                           => carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1748:       Valid domain:       integers in [1, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a with value >= 1.
 1749: 
 1750:     DOMAIN -- offset:
 1751:       EP absent:          no rows skipped; result starts from row 0.
 1752:       BVA min valid = 0:  no rows skipped (zero is a valid non-negative integer).
 1753:       BVA offset=1:       first row skipped; result starts from row 1.
 1754:       BVA offset=N-1:     N-1 rows skipped; only the last row returned.
 1755:       BVA offset=N:       all N rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1756:       BVA offset>N:       all rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1757:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1758:                           => carp + ignored (no rows skipped).
 1759:       Valid domain:       integers in [0, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a.
 1760: 
 1761:     distinct => 1          -- deduplicate rows after the merge (see columns below)
 1762:     columns  => \@cols     -- arrayref of column names to project and deduplicate on;
 1763:                               used together with distinct => 1
 1764: 
 1765:     DOMAIN -- distinct:
 1766:       EP absent or 0:     no deduplication; all merged rows are returned.
 1767:       EP 1:               duplicates are removed (see below); first occurrence wins.
 1768:       EP truthy (not 1):  treated as truthy; deduplication is applied.
 1769:       Effect:             on the array backend, a Perl seen-hash keyed on the
 1770:                           selected column values is used; first occurrence wins.
 1771:                           On the SQLite backend, SELECT DISTINCT is used.
 1772: 
 1773:     DOMAIN -- columns:
 1774:       EP absent/undef:    when distinct is set, deduplication is on all columns
 1775:                           and full rows are returned.
 1776:       EP []:              empty arrayref treated the same as absent.
 1777:       EP valid:           arrayref of column names present in the merged view;
 1778:                           result rows are projected to those columns only, and
 1779:                           deduplication is on the projected values.
 1780:       EP with distinct=0: columns is ignored when distinct is absent or false.
 1781:       Note:               unknown column names in the list are silently ignored
 1782:                           at projection time; the row may lack those keys.
 1783: 
 1784: =head4 Output
 1785: 
 1786:     Arrayref of hashrefs; one hashref per qualifying merged row.
 1787:     Sorted ascending by join_column by default; caller-controlled via sort_by.
 1788:     At most C<limit> rows when limit is given; the first C<offset> rows are
 1789:     skipped when offset is given.
 1790:     Returns a reference to an empty array when no rows match.
 1791: 
 1792: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1793: 
 1794:     # All rows from both databases
 1795:     my $all = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1796: 
 1797:     # Only rows where the 'tier' column (from the loyalty database)
 1798:     # equals 'gold' -- the criterion is routed to the right database
 1799:     my $vip = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1800: 
 1801:     # Operator hashref: score > 80
 1802:     my $high = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1803: 
 1804:     # Access each merged row
 1805:     for my $row (@{$vip}) {
 1806:         printf "%-10s tier=%-8s score=%d\n",
 1807:             $row->{entry}, $row->{tier}, $row->{score} // 0;
 1808:     }
 1809: 
 1810:     # Distinct tier values across all rows (project to one column, deduplicate)
 1811:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1812:     # Returns e.g. [{ tier => 'gold' }, { tier => 'silver' }, { tier => 'bronze' }]
 1813:     # regardless of how many rows exist for each tier
 1814: 
 1815:     # Distinct region+tier combinations
 1816:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1817:         distinct => 1,
 1818:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1819:     );
 1820: 
 1821:     # Distinct on all columns (remove fully-duplicate merged rows)
 1822:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1823: 
 1824: =head3 MESSAGES
 1825: 
 1826:     warn_unknown_column (carp)
 1827:         -- A criterion key names a column not present in any component database;
 1828:            the criterion is silently dropped and all rows are returned.
 1829:     error_join_criterion (croak)
 1830:         -- A criterion hashref contains a join => key (DA SQL-JOIN syntax).
 1831:            Use a separate DA object for the joined table instead.
 1832:     sort_by column unknown (carp)
 1833:         -- The column given in sort_by is not in the merged view; the result
 1834:            is returned in the default join_column ascending order instead.
 1835:     sort_by direction invalid (carp)
 1836:         -- The direction given in sort_by is not 'ASC' or 'DESC'; ASC is used.
 1837:     limit invalid (carp)
 1838:         -- The value given for limit is not a positive integer; it is ignored.
 1839:     offset invalid (carp)
 1840:         -- The value given for offset is not a non-negative integer; it is ignored.
 1841:     operator-unsupported (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1842:         -- An operator hashref key is not in the supported set (>, <, >=, <=,
 1843:            !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE, IS NULL, IS NOT NULL, IN, NOT IN); the
 1844:            individual operator term is dropped from the WHERE clause (other
 1845:            operators in the same hashref still apply).
 1846:     IN/NOT IN wrong value type (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1847:         -- An IN or NOT IN criterion was given a non-arrayref value; the operator
 1848:            term is skipped.
 1849:     error_sqlite_connect (croak, SQLite/auto path only)
 1850:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created; check tmpdir
 1851:            permissions and available disk space.
 1852: 
 1853: =cut
 1854: 
 1855: sub selectall_arrayref {
 1856: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1857: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1858: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1859: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1860: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1861: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1862: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1863: 	return $self->_joined_query($params,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1864: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1865: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1866: }
 1867: 
 1868: =head2 selectall_array
 1869: 
 1870: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1871: 
 1872:     my @rows = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1873: 
 1874:     # Scalar context: only the first matching row
 1875:     my $first = $join->selectall_array(entry => 'C001');
 1876: 
 1877:     # Distinct column projection (list context)
 1878:     my @tiers = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1879: 
 1880:     # Distinct on all columns
 1881:     my @deduped = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1);
 1882: 
 1883: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1884: 
 1885: In list context returns a list of merged hashrefs -- the same rows that
 1886: C<selectall_arrayref> would return, just as a flat list rather than an
 1887: arrayref.
 1888: 
 1889: In scalar context returns only the first matching hashref (or C<undef> if
 1890: nothing matches).
 1891: 
 1892: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1893: 
 1894: =head4 Input
 1895: 
 1896:     Same as selectall_arrayref.
 1897: 
 1898: =head4 Output
 1899: 
 1900:     List context:   list of hashrefs (may be empty).
 1901:     Scalar context: single hashref or undef.
 1902: 
 1903: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1904: 
 1905:     my @all = $join->selectall_array();
 1906:     print scalar @all, " rows\n";
 1907: 
 1908:     # First gold-tier customer only
 1909:     my $first_vip = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1910:     print $first_vip->{name}, "\n" if defined $first_vip;
 1911: 
 1912: =head3 MESSAGES
 1913: 
 1914: Same messages as C<selectall_arrayref>.
 1915: 
 1916: =cut
 1917: 
 1918: sub selectall_array {
 1919: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1920: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1921: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1922: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1923: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1924: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1925: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1926: 	my $rows     = $self->_joined_query($params,
 1927: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1928: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1929: 	return wantarray ? @{$rows} : $rows->[0];

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1930: }
 1931:

 1935: callers written against C<Database::Abstraction>'s deprecated API.  Use
 1936: C<selectall_arrayref> in new code.
 1937: 
 1938: =cut

 1948: Deprecated alias for L</selectall_array>, present for compatibility with

 1951: 
 1952: =cut

 1955: 	my $self = shift;
 1956: 	carp 'Database::Join::selectall_hash is deprecated; use selectall_array';

 1959: 
 1960: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 1961: 
 1962: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1963: 
 1964:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
 1965:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref('C001');   # positional shorthand
 1966: 
 1967: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1968:

 2023:

 2025: rows are counted in Perl.  On the SQLite backend, a C<SELECT COUNT(*)> SQL
 2026: query is executed against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row fetch.

 2065: =head2 aggregate
 2066: 
 2067: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2068: 
 2069:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2070:         by       => ['tier'],
 2071:         measures => [
 2072:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' },
 2073:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'   },
 2074:         ],
 2075:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
 2076:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2077:         limit    => 10,
 2078:     );
 2079:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
 2080: 
 2081: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2082: 
 2083: Groups the merged view by one or more published column names and computes
 2084: aggregate measures over each group.
 2085: 
 2086: On the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) and the C<auto> backend,
 2087: the aggregation is pushed into the existing SQLite cache as a C<GROUP BY>
 2088: query - rows never pass through Perl.  The C<auto> backend always takes this
 2089: path for aggregation, skipping the row-count threshold probe (aggregation
 2090: makes the row count irrelevant).
 2091: 
 2092: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), merged rows are fetched in
 2093: full and folded in Perl.  This materialises the complete matching result set.
 2094: 
 2095: The two paths return the same rows for the same input, except for two
 2096: documented differences: C<min>/C<max> on a column declared C<TEXT> but
 2097: storing numbers (SQL compares lexically; Perl detects numerics), and
 2098: C<sum>/C<avg> with non-numeric values (SQL coerces to 0.0; Perl skips and
 2099: warns via C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>).
 2100: 
 2101: C<Database::Join> has no C<no_fixate> and no CHI cache (unlike
 2102: C<Database::Abstraction>); aggregation results are not cached between calls.
 2103: 
 2104: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2105: 
 2106: =head4 Input
 2107: 
 2108: Reserved keys extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria:
 2109: 
 2110:     by       => arrayref of published column names (default: [] = grand total)
 2111:     measures => arrayref of measure hashrefs (required, non-empty)
 2112:     having   => hashref of post-aggregation criteria
 2113:     sort_by  => 'col' or ['col', 'DESC']
 2114:     limit    => positive integer
 2115:     offset   => non-negative integer
 2116: 
 2117: Every other key is pre-aggregation criteria routed exactly as
 2118: C<selectall_arrayref> routes criteria.
 2119: 
 2120: Measure hashref keys:
 2121: 
 2122:     fn  => function name (sum|avg|min|max|count|count_distinct), case-insensitive
 2123:     col => published column name (required for all functions except bare count)
 2124:     as  => result key (required; must match C</^\w+\z/>; unique; not a by name)
 2125: 
 2126: C<having> uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>:
 2127: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS> (E<gt>, E<lt>, E<gt>=, E<lt>=, !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE),
 2128: C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS> (IN, NOT IN), C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS> (IS NULL, IS NOT NULL),
 2129: a bare scalar (= ?), and C<-or>/C<-and> groupings.  Write C<-between> as
 2130: two operators and C<LIKE> explicitly.
 2131: 
 2132: =head4 Output
 2133: 
 2134: Always an arrayref of hashrefs (never C<undef>).  C<[]> when no group
 2135: qualifies and C<by> is non-empty.  When C<by> is omitted or C<[]> and no
 2136: rows match, returns one all-normalised row (grand total of zero).
 2137: 
 2138: Key order per row: C<by> columns in declaration order, then measure aliases
 2139: in declaration order.
 2140: 
 2141: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2142: 
 2143:     # Total score and row count per tier, highest total first
 2144:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2145:         by       => ['tier'],
 2146:         measures => [
 2147:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
 2148:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
 2149:         ],
 2150:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2151:     );
 2152:     for my $r (@{$rows}) {
 2153:         printf "%s: total=%s n=%s\n", $r->{tier}, $r->{total}, $r->{n};
 2154:     }
 2155: 
 2156: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2157: 
 2158:     1. get_params(undef, @args) -- hashref or kv-pairs only (no positional key)
 2159:     2. croak error_join_criterion if 'join =>' present
 2160:     3. _validate_aggregate_spec extracts by/measures/having/sort_by/limit/offset
 2161:     4. array backend:
 2162:          rows   = _joined_query_array(remaining criteria, no sort/limit/offset)
 2163:          groups = _fold_aggregate(rows, spec)
 2164:          groups = [grep { _match_criteria($_, having) } @groups] if having
 2165:          _normalise_aggregate(groups, spec.measures)
 2166:          _sort_and_page(groups, spec)
 2167:        sqlite/auto backend:
 2168:          _sqlite_aggregate(remaining criteria, spec)
 2169:     5. return groups
 2170: 
 2171: =head3 MESSAGES
 2172: 
 2173: C<error_aggregate_measures_required>, C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>,
 2174: C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_col_required>,
 2175: C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_as_required>,
 2176: C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>, C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>,
 2177: C<error_aggregate_as_collision>, C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>,
 2178: C<error_aggregate_having_key>, C<error_aggregate_having_operator>,
 2179: C<warn_aggregate_sort_key>, C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>.
 2180: 
 2181: =cut
 2182:

 2214: 
 2215: =head2 each_row
 2216: 
 2217: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2218: 
 2219:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { my ($row) = @_; ... });
 2220:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, tier => 'gold');
 2221:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100);
 2222: 
 2223: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2224: 
 2225: Calls C<\&callback> once for every row in the merged view that matches the
 2226: given criteria, then returns the total count of rows visited.
 2227: 
 2228: Accepts the same criteria, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset> parameters as
 2229: C<selectall_arrayref>.
 2230: 
 2231: B<Note on memory usage>: C<Database::Join> always materialises the complete
 2232: merged result set before invoking the callback, because merging rows from
 2233: multiple independent sources requires that every source be queried and
 2234: cross-referenced first.  True constant-memory streaming (as C<Database::Abstraction>
 2235: provides on single-source SQL queries) is not possible at the join layer.
 2236: For genuinely large result sets use the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt>
 2237: 'sqlite'>), which keeps peak RAM to approximately one times the source data
 2238: size rather than three.
 2239: 
 2240: Any exception thrown inside the callback propagates to the caller after the
 2241: current row; subsequent rows are not visited.
 2242: 
 2243: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2244: 
 2245: =head4 Input
 2246: 
 2247:     \&callback   Positional coderef (required).
 2248:                  Called as $callback->($row_hashref) for each merged row.
 2249: 
 2250:     Additional arguments follow the same calling conventions as
 2251:     selectall_arrayref: no args, a single scalar join-column value,
 2252:     or key-value criteria pairs (including sort_by, limit, offset).
 2253: 
 2254: =head4 Output
 2255: 
 2256:     Non-negative integer: the count of rows for which the callback was invoked.
 2257: 
 2258: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2259: 
 2260:     # Print every gold-tier customer's name, sorted by name
 2261:     my $count = $join->each_row(
 2262:         sub { my ($row) = @_; print "$row->{name}\n" },
 2263:         tier    => 'gold',
 2264:         sort_by => 'name',
 2265:     );
 2266:     print "$count gold-tier customers\n";
 2267: 
 2268:     # Accumulate without holding the full result
 2269:     my $total_score = 0;
 2270:     $join->each_row(sub { $total_score += $_[0]->{score} // 0 });
 2271: 
 2272: =head3 MESSAGES
 2273: 
 2274:     error_invalid_callback (croak)
 2275:         -- First argument is not a code reference.
 2276: 
 2277: =cut
 2278:

 2295: =head2 dbi_source
 2296: 
 2297: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2298: 
 2299:     # Use a Database::Join object as a zero-copy SQLite source inside a
 2300:     # parent Database::Join, giving the parent ATTACHed-speed access to
 2301:     # the child's joined data without iterating through Perl.
 2302:     my $child  = Database::Join->new(databases => [$da, $db], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2303:     my $parent = Database::Join->new(databases => [$child, $dc], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2304: 
 2305: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2306: 
 2307: Returns a hashref C<< { dbh => $sqlite_dbh, table => '_dj_result' } >> that
 2308: allows a parent C<Database::Join> (or any other caller that understands the
 2309: C<dbi_source()> interface) to ATTACH the child's temporary SQLite database
 2310: file and query the materialised join result directly via SQL, without routing
 2311: rows through Perl.
 2312: 
 2313: The first call builds the SQLite cache (if not already current) and
 2314: materialises the full join result - with C<filters> applied but no query-time
 2315: criteria - into a real table named C<_dj_result> inside the cache file.
 2316: Subsequent calls within the same cache cycle reuse the existing table.
 2317: 
 2318: Returns C<undef> when the backend is C<'array'> (no SQLite file exists).
 2319: 
 2320: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2321: 
 2322: =head4 Input
 2323: 
 2324:     None.
 2325: 
 2326: =head4 Output
 2327: 
 2328:     On the SQLite/auto backend:
 2329:       Hashref with keys:
 2330:         dbh   => DBI handle to the child's temporary SQLite database file.
 2331:         table => '_dj_result'  (the materialized join table inside that file).
 2332:     On the array backend:
 2333:       undef
 2334: 
 2335: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2336: 
 2337:     # The parent automatically ATTACHes the child's SQLite file and queries
 2338:     # _dj_result for zero-copy composable nested joins.
 2339:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
 2340:         databases   => [$customers, $loyalty],
 2341:         join_column => 'entry',
 2342:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2343:     );
 2344:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
 2345:         databases   => [$inner, $scores],
 2346:         join_column => 'entry',
 2347:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2348:     );
 2349:     my $rows = $outer->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 2350: 
 2351: =head3 MESSAGES
 2352: 
 2353:     error_sqlite_connect (croak)
 2354:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created.
 2355: 
 2356: =cut

 2530: =head2 updated
 2531: 
 2532: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2533: 
 2534:     my $ts = $join->updated();
 2535: 
 2536: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2537: 
 2538: Returns the Unix timestamp of the most recent modification across all
 2539: component databases.  This is the maximum of all individual C<updated()>
 2540: return values.
 2541: 
 2542: Use this to implement simple cache-invalidation logic: if C<updated()>
 2543: has advanced since your last snapshot, re-query.
 2544: 
 2545: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2546: 
 2547: =head4 Input
 2548: 
 2549:     None.
 2550: 
 2551: =head4 Output
 2552: 
 2553:     Unix timestamp (positive integer).
 2554: 
 2555: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2556: 
 2557:     my $last_modified = $join->updated();
 2558:     if ($last_modified > $my_cache_timestamp) {
 2559:         $my_cache = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 2560:         $my_cache_timestamp = $last_modified;
 2561:     }
 2562: 
 2563: =head3 MESSAGES
 2564: 
 2565: C<updated()> does not emit any warnings or errors.  Component databases that do
 2566: not implement C<updated()>, or whose C<updated()> throws, are silently skipped;
 2567: only defined return values contribute to the maximum.  If no component database
 2568: implements C<updated()>, C<undef> is returned (same as C<List::Util::max> on an
 2569: empty list).
 2570: 
 2571: =cut
 2572: 
 2573: sub updated {
●2574 → 2576 → 2581 2574: 	my ($self) = @_;

 2577: 		my $ts;
 2578: 		do { local $@; $ts = eval { $db->updated() } };
 2579: 		push @timestamps, $ts if defined $ts;
 2580: 	}
 2581: 	return max(@timestamps);

 2584: =head2 set_logger
 2585: 
 2586: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2587: 
 2588:     $join->set_logger($log);
 2589:     $join->set_logger(logger => $log);   # named-pair form also accepted
 2590: 
 2591: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2592: 
 2593: Attaches a new logger object to the join and propagates it to every component
 2594: database.  The logger is used for diagnostic output by all component databases.
 2595: 
 2596: Non-blessed values (a log-level string such as C<"debug">, a filename, or a
 2597: code reference) are wrapped in C<Log::Abstraction->new(...)> automatically,
 2598: matching the behaviour of C<Database::Abstraction::set_logger>.
 2599: 
 2600: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2601: 
 2602: =head4 Input
 2603: 
 2604:     logger  Positional or named: a logger object, log-level string, filename,
 2605:             or code reference (required).  Non-blessed values are wrapped in
 2606:             Log::Abstraction automatically.
 2607: 
 2608: =head4 Output
 2609: 
 2610:     Returns C<$self> for method chaining.
 2611: 
 2612: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2613: 
 2614:     # Log::Any is used here as an example; any object that implements
 2615:     # debug() and info() (or whichever methods your component databases
 2616:     # call internally) works equally well.
 2617:     use Log::Any qw($log);
 2618: 
 2619:     my $join = Database::Join->new(databases => [$db1, $db2], join_column => 'entry');
 2620:     $join->set_logger($log);
 2621:     # $log is now used by $join and by $db1 and $db2
 2622: 
 2623:     # Named-pair form (mirrors Database::Abstraction API):
 2624:     $join->set_logger(logger => $log);
 2625: 
 2626: =head3 MESSAGES
 2627: 
 2628:     (croak) Usage: set_logger(logger => $logger)
 2629:         -- Called with an undefined argument.  Pass a valid logger object.
 2630: 
 2631: =cut

 2651: =head2 add_database
 2652: 
 2653: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2654: 
 2655:     # Positional: database object as first argument
 2656:     $join->add_database($db);
 2657: 
 2658:     # Named: equivalent to the above
 2659:     $join->add_database(database => $db);
 2660: 
 2661:     # With options (mixed positional + named)
 2662:     $join->add_database($db, remove_columns => ['internal_id']);
 2663:     $join->add_database($db, join_column    => 'local_key_name');
 2664:     $join->add_database($db, filter         => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2665: 
 2666:     # Chainable
 2667:     $join->add_database($db1)->add_database($db2, remove_columns => ['notes']);
 2668: 
 2669: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2670: 
 2671: Adds one more C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object to the logical view
 2672: and immediately updates the column-ownership index.
 2673: 
 2674: After the call, all query methods return rows that include columns from the
 2675: newly added database, and criteria on those new columns are routed to it
 2676: automatically.
 2677: 
 2678: When a column name in the new database already exists in an earlier database,
 2679: the new database becomes the authoritative source for that column
 2680: (last-database-wins, the same rule that applies at construction time).
 2681: 
 2682: The join-column must be present in the new database (or declared via
 2683: C<join_column>).  The logger is propagated to the new database if one is set.
 2684: 
 2685: C<add_database> is the runtime equivalent of listing the database in the
 2686: C<databases> array to C<new>.  The optional C<join_column> parameter is
 2687: equivalent to a C<join_map> entry; the optional C<filter> parameter is
 2688: equivalent to a C<filters> entry.
 2689: 
 2690: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2691: 
 2692: =head4 Input
 2693: 
 2694:     database       => { type => 'object',   required => 1 }
 2695:                       # A Database::Abstraction subclass instance.
 2696:                       #
 2697:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   blessed object that passes
 2698:                       #                       isa('Database::Abstraction').
 2699:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-reference, unblessed ref, wrong class,
 2700:                       #                       or non-reference non-key scalar (the guard at
 2701:                       #                       the top of add_database rejects it with
 2702:                       #                       error_invalid_db before validate_strict runs).
 2703: 
 2704:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
 2705:                       # The name of the join key in THIS new database,
 2706:                       # when it differs from the canonical join_column.
 2707:                       #
 2708:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that exists as a column in the
 2709:                       #                       new database.
 2710:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: string absent from the new database's columns()
 2711:                       #                       => croak error_join_col_missing.
 2712: 
 2713:     filter         => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
 2714:                       # Permanent criteria for this database only.
 2715:                       # Same format as selectall_arrayref.
 2716:                       #
 2717:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of criteria (may be {} for no-op).
 2718:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  no permanent filter applied; all rows visible.
 2719:                       # DOMAIN -- Key-set:    a non-empty filter makes this DB an inner-join
 2720:                       #                       partner regardless of the outer join_type.
 2721: 
 2722:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
 2723:                       # Column names from this database to hide.
 2724:                       #
 2725:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of strings; non-existent columns silently
 2726:                       #                       ignored; empty [] is a safe no-op.
 2727:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2728: 
 2729: =head4 Output
 2730: 
 2731:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2732: 
 2733: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2734: 
 2735:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2736:         databases   => [ $customers ],
 2737:         join_column => 'entry',
 2738:     );
 2739: 
 2740:     # Add loyalty data; hide internal columns from it
 2741:     $join->add_database($loyalty, remove_columns => ['audit_ts']);
 2742: 
 2743:     # Add score data; only include rows with score > 60
 2744:     $join->add_database($scores, filter => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2745: 
 2746:     # Add a database whose join key has a different local name
 2747:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'state_code');
 2748: 
 2749:     # All three options combined, and chained
 2750:     $join->add_database($db4,
 2751:         join_column    => 'ref_id',
 2752:         filter         => { active => 1 },
 2753:         remove_columns => ['legacy_col'],
 2754:     );
 2755: 
 2756: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2757: 
 2758:     determine the new database's index (length of current _dbs array)
 2759:     extract the database object from positional or named argument
 2760:     croak if it is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2761:     register join_column alias in _join_map if different from canonical
 2762:     register filter in _filters if provided
 2763:     fetch column list from the new database
 2764:     croak if the join key is missing from the new database
 2765:     append the new database to _dbs and _db_cols
 2766:     update _col_db: for each new column, point it at the new index
 2767:         (last-database-wins; skip removed columns and the local join alias)
 2768:     invalidate _col_cache and _schema_cache
 2769:     propagate logger if set
 2770:     apply remove_columns if provided
 2771:     return $self
 2772: 
 2773: =head3 MESSAGES
 2774: 
 2775:     error_invalid_db          -- argument is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2776:     error_join_col_missing    -- join_column not found in the new database
 2777:     warn_schema_type_mismatch -- (carp) the new database has a shared column whose type
 2778:                                  differs from the type already in the view; use
 2779:                                  collision_prefix to preserve both values
 2780: 
 2781: =cut

 2889: =head2 remove_column
 2890: 
 2891: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2892: 
 2893:     $join->remove_column('email');
 2894: 
 2895:     # Chainable
 2896:     $join->remove_column('internal_id')->remove_column('audit_ts');
 2897: 
 2898: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2899: 
 2900: Permanently hides a column from the merged view.  After this call:
 2901: 
 2902: =over 4
 2903: 
 2904: =item *
 2905: 
 2906: The column does not appear in C<columns()> or C<schema()>.
 2907: 
 2908: =item *
 2909: 
 2910: Returned row hashrefs do not contain the column key.
 2911: 
 2912: =item *
 2913: 
 2914: Any query criterion that references the removed column is silently dropped
 2915: (with a C<carp> warning).
 2916: 
 2917: =back
 2918: 
 2919: The C<join_column> cannot be removed; attempting to do so will C<croak>.
 2920: Removing a column that does not exist in any database is silently ignored
 2921: (the call is idempotent and safe).  The C<columns()> and C<schema()>
 2922: memoisation caches are cleared automatically.
 2923: 
 2924: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2925: 
 2926: =head4 Input
 2927: 
 2928:     $col    Positional string: the column name to remove.
 2929: 
 2930:             DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string; non-existent columns are silently
 2931:                                   ignored (idempotent call, returns $self).
 2932:             DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column value => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2933:             DOMAIN -- BVA:        undef and '' are explicit no-ops (returns $self).
 2934:                                   These are below the minimum meaningful string
 2935:                                   length and are handled without any warning.
 2936: 
 2937: =head4 Output
 2938: 
 2939:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2940: 
 2941: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2942: 
 2943:     # Hide private fields immediately after construction
 2944:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2945:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 2946:         join_column => 'entry',
 2947:     )->remove_column('email')
 2948:      ->remove_column('internal_notes');
 2949: 
 2950:     # Verify they are gone
 2951:     my $cols = $join->columns();
 2952:     # 'email' and 'internal_notes' are absent
 2953: 
 2954: =head3 MESSAGES
 2955: 
 2956:     error_remove_join_col -- attempt to remove the join_column itself
 2957: 
 2958: =cut

 3027: 
 3028: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 3029: 
 3030: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a visible column name performs
 3031: a column lookup across the merged view.
 3032: 
 3033: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 3034: 
 3035:     # Scalar context: value from the first matching row
 3036:     my $name  = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
 3037: 
 3038:     # List context: values from every matching row
 3039:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
 3040: 
 3041:     # With a positional join-key argument (when join_column is 'entry')
 3042:     my $score = $join->score('C001');
 3043: 
 3044: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 3045: 
 3046: AUTOLOAD routes the call to the appropriate component database by looking up
 3047: the column name in the internal column-ownership index.
 3048: 
 3049: When either C<join_map> or C<filters> is active, AUTOLOAD performs a full
 3050: join query instead of delegating directly to the owning database.  This is
 3051: necessary because:
 3052: 
 3053: =over 4
 3054: 
 3055: =item *
 3056: 
 3057: With C<join_map>, the owning database's primary key may differ from the
 3058: canonical join key used in the call arguments.
 3059: 
 3060: =item *
 3061: 
 3062: With C<filters>, bypassing the join would return rows that the filter is
 3063: meant to exclude.
 3064: 
 3065: =back
 3066: 
 3067: In list context, every matching merged row contributes one value to the
 3068: returned list.  In scalar context, only the first row's value is returned.
 3069: 
 3070: Calling a method whose name begins with C<_> (a private method) via AUTOLOAD
 3071: will C<croak> with a clear error message rather than being silently ignored.
 3072: 
 3073: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3074: 
 3075:     # Lookup a single customer's name (scalar context)
 3076:     my $name = $join->name('C001');   # 'C001' maps to entry => 'C001'
 3077:     print "Name: $name\n";
 3078: 
 3079:     # Get every tier value in the view (list context)
 3080:     my @all_tiers = $join->tier();
 3081:     my %freq;
 3082:     $freq{$_}++ for @all_tiers;
 3083: 
 3084:     # join_map active: AUTOLOAD runs a full join so the criteria are
 3085:     # translated correctly between the canonical and local key names.

 3090: 
 3091:     extract column name from $AUTOLOAD

 3109:     (croak) Database::Join: unknown column 'NAME'
 3110:         -- Method name does not match any visible column in the merged view.

 3412: # _joined_query_array( \%params ) -> \@merged_rows
 3413: #
 3414: # Purpose: Core in-memory join algorithm.  Partitions criteria, fetches per-database
 3415: #          results, resolves the key set, and merges rows.
 3416: #
 3417: # Key-set resolution (applied for each secondary database after the primary):
 3418: #
 3419: #   If the database had criteria in this query call (after base filter overlay),
 3420: #   it acts as an INNER-JOIN partner: only keys present in its filtered result
 3421: #   survive.  This gives WHERE-clause semantics even under a LEFT join.
 3422: #
 3423: #   If the database had NO effective criteria:
 3424: #     inner  -> intersect  (standard inner join)
 3425: #     left   -> no change  (primary defines the key set)
 3426: #     outer  -> union      (all keys from any database)
 3427: #
 3428: # Row merge: for each qualifying primary row, secondary rows are overlaid in
 3429: # index order.  For duplicate columns, later databases win.  Local join-key
 3430: # aliases are renamed to the canonical join_column before merging.
 3431: # Removed columns are deleted from every merged row.

 5184: # _copy_criteria( \%criteria ) -> \%copy
 5185: # Purpose: Return a two-level deep copy of a single criteria hashref so that
 5186: #          post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot change the
 5187: #          stored filter.  Operator sub-hashrefs (e.g. { '>' => 80 }) are
 5188: #          shallow-copied one additional level, matching the broadcast-copy
 5189: #          idiom used in _partition_criteria for join-column criteria.
 5190: # Entry:   $criteria is a hashref (may be undef).
 5191: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5192: sub _copy_criteria {
 5193: 	my ($criteria) = @_;
 5194: 	return {} unless $criteria && %{$criteria};
 5195: 	return {
 5196: 		map {
 5197: 			$_ => ref($criteria->{$_}) eq 'HASH'
 5198: 				? { %{ $criteria->{$_} } }  # shallow-copy operator sub-hashref
 5199: 				: $criteria->{$_}
 5200: 		} keys %{$criteria}
 5201: 	};
 5202: }
